Customs consultants are in line with the intensity of relations with foreign countries and the increase in foreign trade volumes, and are charged with providing answers and solutions to the questions and problems of our citizens, Turkish companies abroad and foreign citizens coming to Turkey, while informing them of Turkish customs regulations and practices. Dear MPs, our Ministry is making necessary changes in customs and trade legislation in accordance with the changing conditions of the day and the needs of those who benefit from our services. Again, 77 million consumers in our country have been granted advanced rights, with regulations made in favour of consumers. We're protecting the rights of 77 million consumers with a total of 1,003 consumer arbitration committees in all of our cities and boroughs. In the first eight months of 2014, 91 per cent of the total 642.226 applications came in favour of our consumers. We've activated the consumer information system. We also took important steps to address the problems of the co-operatives at the credit point. Cutbacks for co-operatives were 9 per cent in 2002, while we narrowed it down to 3 per cent in 2014. We also made significant improvements in credit costs by reducing interest rates from 47 per cent to 4 per cent during the same period. At the end of 2002, 402 co-ops could use loans, but as of today, the number rose to 851. Again, at the end of 2002, 63,520 tradesmen and artists used 154 million liras, while the total loan to 315,200 trades and artists has increased by about 80 times to 12.2 billion dollars. Mr. President, the high-value lawmakers make it necessary for us, as a country, to constantly change the conditions of trade in the world, to modernise our development, our administrative and physical structure. Besides local measures to maintain customs services much faster and more effectively, to reduce the waiting at border crossings to a minimum, new customs gates and managerships are being opened in the needed areas, and the existing doors are being renovated. Again, for modernisation of customs labs, the work volume of 23 customs labs has been assessed according to the physical structure, number of staff, technical equipment, and the laboratory has been narrowed down to 6. Six customs labs were invested in $30m of Turkish lira, including $24m from the budget and $6m from the European Union budget. Dear lawmakers, as Ministry of Customs and Trade, our priority is to facilitate legal trade. The facilitating of legal trade allows more effective fighting of illegal trade, reducing formalities due to customs procedures, and reducing customs processing costs to minimum. Our Ministry of Customs and Trade is weighing on projects that are based on legislation, employment policies, development of physical conditions and the development of electronic systems to achieve economic goals in the vision of our 2023 vision. On this occasion, I would like to wish you the best of the 2015 budget and the work done for our country.

As you know, the rules of competition are regulations in many countries around the world, especially developed countries. In most countries, public authorities have been established for effective implementation of these rules, which are considered indispensable for the healthy functioning of the market economy. In our country, the rule of Article 167 of our Constitution has given the state the task of taking the necessary measures for the healthy and regular functioning of markets, monopoly and cartelization. The recent developments in Turkey's economy have been evident. In the early years of the Competitive Association, in 1998, our country, which had an economic size of around $270 billion, had largely completed its process of liberalisation in its economic structure, becoming one of the world's leading economies with an estimated $820 billion U.S. income in 2013, strengthening its position among the G20 countries that made up 85 per cent of the world's economy in 2015. The role of competing rules of competition is greater in the fact that the national economy works better with all institutions and rules of the more competitive, more open and free market economy to the outside world. The Competitiveness Agency, which is trying to eliminate artificial interference outside the market mechanism in the economy, continues to work successfully in this direction. The Competitiveness Agency has provided an important deterrent against possible competitive violations, with decisions that it has made in cases where competitive conditions have so far been broken by attempts. By offering a fair environment to the consumer and to the manufacturer, the citizen was tried to obtain better quality, plenty of choice products and services. If the conditions of competition were not observed, it would be no surprise that we would no longer encounter giants in the market who are consulting, lowering quality, threatening competitive order, and trying to monopolize and control the market and swallow small businesses, not allowing new firms to enter the market. In short, the square would be reserved for companies who stole from the welfare of society and from its presence by illegal acts. In the current case, our consumers can make different choices in line with need, demand, pleasure, and budget, and can easily find any product or service in the market. Our entrepreneurs can compete, produce, and plan their future investments in any field they want. Here we have to ignore the activities of the Competition Agency in order to ensure that the markets work well and orderly for everyone from its producer to its consumers. The Competitive Association has made decisions by conducting investigations, investigations and investigations of attempts in various sectors based on applications that have been sent to it from its establishment to date, or by self-instruction. Competitiveness and initiatives are subject to administrative fines, up to 10 per cent of their turnouts, and for executives or employees with a decisive impact on violations, up to 5 per cent of this sentence. In a nutshell, the Competitiveness Agency has undersigned important decisions and practices that remove obstacles to competition in the markets, which provide economic benefit to consumers. In this process, the institution has taken care to implement a transparent, fair and impartial practice that captures progress in the European Union and other international standards. As a matter of fact, both in EU progress reports and international institutional work, such as OECD, praises Turkey's point of reference to advanced competitive law norms. With all of these qualities, the Competition Agency will continue its activities with determination by targeting competitive markets that will allow consumers to buy better quality products at lower prices. I wish our budget for 2015 to be good for our country and for our beloved nation, and I salute your grand delegation.

tbmm-2014-12-16sit01spe0040par0305	Mr. President, I have made a commitment to the budget of the Turkish Water Institute for Forestry and Water Affairs of the Central Management Budget Act of 2015. Members of parliament, the Turkish Water Institute is a special budgetary organisation founded in 2011 by the Constitution of Law No. 658, under the State Department of Forestry and Water Affairs. I want to thank Mr. Veysel Eroglu, our minister who worked on this because he's become an important organization. The aim of the organisation is to contribute to the solution of global water problems, to direct work towards the future on water, to contribute with new ideas and initiatives to the creation of our country's sustainable water policies and strategies. At the same time, the institute was founded on goals such as organizing national and international meetings and educational programmes, supporting global cooperation in water, developing and implementing plans and projects that open global windows to water policies, representing our country on international platforms, and continuing to work in that direction. Congressmans, in our globalized world, water policies also exceed borders. It is not possible to ignore the world's developments. Turkey has become the leader of water in its region, thanks to its mega-projects, its growing manpower and vast knowledge. Turkey's geographical position is one of the key factors in establishing a strong communication network in Central Asia, the Middle East and Eastern Europe. Turkey's Water Institute for Forestry and Water Affairs is working to boost technical cooperation in the region to address regional water problems, and has many joint projects and activities in its area, especially with the elite institutions and organisations around the world. In March 2009, our country organised the 5th of the world's water forums, which included over 30,000 people from 192 countries. This forum, which happens every three years, is known as the most important event in the water area in the world. The 5th World Water Forum Secretariat of Turkey's Water Institute was formed as a think tank under the forest and waterworks ministry's roof in an effort to implement its scientific experience and vision for water. The great success of the 5th World Water Forum has taken Turkey to a new position where it has a global power over water. The multi-directional collection of information provided by this important forum laid the foundation of the Water Institute within the Ministry. In the past, our country has remained only observer status in water-field meetings, while it has now become the country in power to participate in international meetings. The activities of the Water Institute are not limited to work in the international arena. Our country is producing scientific literature, such as reports and articles, to ensure sustainable use of water resources, and is working with universities to model underground and above-ground water sources. Congressmans, as we all know, our country is not a water-rich country. In a century when global warming threatens the world, we need to look for ways not to reduce our available amount of water, 1,500 cubic metres per person. As it is known, countries with the amount of water available per person under 1,000 cubic metres are water-poor, 2,000-3. Between 5,000 cubic metres, countries with low water and 8,000 cubic metres and above are considered water-rich countries. Depending on population growth, we too are in danger of entering water-poor countries in 2030. Therefore, in our country, which is not very rich in water resources, we need to take new measures that encourage the austerity of water in all sectors, and continue with existing support. We must always prevent its subterranean and subterranean resources from being contaminated in any way possible. Our agricultural sector, our municipalities and our industrial sector must use water in the most efficient and sustainable way. In addition, the fact that our country is in a semi-dry climate region increases the importance of the Water Institute's work. It is important to increase the quality and quantity of available water, to maintain the sustainability of water protection and use. The Institute organized the D8 Water Co-operation Meeting in February 2013. This meeting was the first meeting of water between the D8 countries. tbmm-2014-12-16sit03spe0153par0094	Mr. President, my fellow congressmen, I would like to inform you on the budget of the Ministry of Customs and Trade under the budget of 2015 and present to you on this occasion some issues regarding the activities and objectives of our Ministry. On this occasion, I salute you all. Dear friends, as the Ministry of Customs and Trade, we have two basic goals. One is the acceleration of customs procedures related to customs procedures, which means accelerating customs in a much faster, much more efficient, efficient, and cost-reduction manner in both imports and export transactions. The second is to ensure that the customs process is accelerated from one side, while the customs process is secured from the other. In a moment, I'll try to tell you exactly what I mean by that. Before we go into these, I'd like to share with you some figures to give you an idea of our ministry's processing volume, its capacity: the number of vehicles entering and exiting our customs as of 2014 is 7,67,000. The number of in-and-out passengers is around 104 million. The total of taxes collected by our ministry is around 68 billion dollars, which is 23 percent of the total tax revenues. The number of export statements that have been processed is 3 million, 34,000 as of January 2014-November figures, and the number of import declarations is 2,178. One of the most important goals I've just tried to express is that all transactions are accelerated in customs. Again, as far as it's concerned, important work has been done since 2003, which has essentially been taking action. For example, at the end of 2002, the rate of export transactions, which took place at the end of 2002, amounted to 3 per cent of the total export process, while today, as of 2014, this rate has increased to 67 per cent. So in one minute, 67 per cent of export operations are concluded. The average export period was two hours and forty-three minutes in 2002, while it was still over ten hours. Again, I would like to share some figures to clarify the issues that have been discussed recently: there are three types of lines at the entrance and exit as customs are being processed. One of them is blue, yellow and red. So according to the level of control, they're classified. A vehicle or object that will pass through the red line, as predicted, is subject to full detection, all aspects of it are controlled, counted, measured, and fully identified. Yellow passes a little faster, blue passes faster. As of December 2014, 7.1 per cent of our export transactions pass through the red line. So the proportion of this practice, which is sometimes controversial and claimed to take time, is 7.1 percent in exports. The same ratio is 18.4 percent in import transactions. Of course, these can vary according to the risk factor of countries, according to the group of goods, according to the group of goods. Of course, our goal is to speed things up. In this framework, one of our most important work at the moment is the reduction of a car to 1. So when a vehicle enters Turkey, a check is conducted at the entrance, the license plate is read, then passed through passport control, then weighed, registered, examined, and then checked as it leaves again. A total of six phases of inspection is in the import stage of a vehicle. Now we're putting them down. The name of the project is already Project One Door. We have reached the end. And we're doing it, hopefully, to start with Sarp and Kaskule. The vehicle or the vehicle that comes in with this will stop on a single platform and all operations will be handled by one person. Actually, we're still in touch with the Home Office. If there's a consensus that the passport control process should be carried out by customs officers, it'll be that way. So six transactions by one person, one platform, one stop, and one person. But if there's no agreement there, it'll still be done at one stop, but there'll be two of them, passport control will be carried out by the Ministry of Internal Affairs. tbmm-2014-12-16sit04spe0250par0237	Thank you, Mayor. I have made a pledge on my own behalf against the State Department of Energy and Natural Resources budget of 2015. I salute you all. Mr. President, valuable lawmakers, research has shown that the construction of thermal plants and HES dams has led to major damage in nature and human life. Obstructing the flow of streams and streams through dams has a negative effect on natural life in the waterbed. Farming and livestock activities along the waterbed also suffer major losses. Because of some projects, we have witnessed floods of villages and towns where people have lived for hundreds of years. With more than 2,000 HES and thousands of dams, Turkey is known to be the country that builds the most HES and dams in Europe. Since HES and dam constructions have no basis for nature's protection, many streams and wetlands are being established. The amount of wetlands lost in Turkey covers a larger area than the Sea of Marmara. In addition to natural life, thousands of years of historical and cultural heritage are buried in the water. For example, the flood of the cities of Hasankeyf and Zeugma, the plunder of Munzur and Periwater Valley's natural fabric, the HES dams that destroyed Black Sea's nature, are only a few of the damage the government's energy policy has caused us. Mr. President, valued lawmakers, the resolution process has been considered by the government as an opportunity to turn Kurdish provinces and regional geography into military bases. The strategic sites that cannot be made into fortresses are being flooded with projects built in the region's cities in parallel with the speeded-up castle constructions. Given the amount of electricity thought to be obtained in the dams, the real goal is not energy production. The dams and HES projects, including Şırnak, Bingöl, Bitlis, Siirt and Diyarbakır, have been accelerated for this purpose. The government is so insistent on these projects that even the state of the courts and the cancellations can't stop the government, unfortunately. For example, studies are being carried out despite the decision of courts related to the Ilisu Dam, which continue to work in Dersim. Mr. President, the esteemed lawmakers, known for their historical and natural beauty, are also being destroyed by the Van geography of my own constituency, HES. 11 HES projects are being implemented in our Van province. There are two HES projects in the lower and upper part of the touristy Muradiye Falls, known in Muradiye district of Van. This project closes the route of the breeding season of the pearl kephali fish, which can only live in Lake Van. The project, which continues with Muradiye Municipality's application for the issue, was sealed by the court due to lack of a license, but the producer is breaking the seals and continuing construction. Again, a HES is expected to be made at the exit point of the spring water, which is identified as the softest water in the world, leaving a cliff at the location of Serekani in the District of Bahçasaray, Van. With this HES, the nature of the Mukus Tea and its source will disappear to see tourists from miles away. A HES is also wanted in the waterbed of Kanispi Falls, located in Çatak district. In Ercish district of Van Province, three HES projects have been tendered and are expected to begin work shortly after. The dams thought to be built with these projects include Zilan Creek and Zilan Plain. As it is known, Zilan Creek is a region with the bones of tens of thousands of citizens who died in the 1930 Zilan massacre. It is unacceptable for the government to carry out dam projects in order to destroy the trails of the massacre, while there are serious demands for a monument to be built in memory of the massacres in this region and the memory of those who have lost their lives. As a result, studies of natural and environmentally friendly solar energy, wind and geothermal energy should be done. The budget must be earmarked for the implementation of the project. Energy investments should now be shifted into this area in a way that will be needed in the future. My regards to the General Assembly. I think the minister found the answer to the solutions to how to do it in the last paragraph. I'm paying my respects again.
